Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

ZS the Coder loves to read the dictionary. He thinks that a word is nice if there exists a substring (contiguous segment of letters) of it of length 26 where each letter of English alphabet appears exactly once. In particular, if the string has length strictly less than 26, no such substring exists and thus it is not nice.

Now, ZS the Coder tells you a word, where some of its letters are missing as he forgot them. He wants to determine if it is possible to fill in the missing letters so that the resulting word is nice. If it is possible, he needs you to find an example of such a word as well. Can you help him?


-----Input-----

The first and only line of the input contains a single string s (1 ≤ |s| ≤ 50 000), the word that ZS the Coder remembers. Each character of the string is the uppercase letter of English alphabet ('A'-'Z') or is a question mark ('?'), where the question marks denotes the letters that ZS the Coder can't remember.


-----Output-----

If there is no way to replace all the question marks with uppercase letters such that the resulting word is nice, then print  - 1 in the only line.

Otherwise, print a string which denotes a possible nice word that ZS the Coder learned. This string should match the string from the input, except for the question marks replaced with uppercase English letters.

If there are multiple solutions, you may print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
ABC??FGHIJK???OPQR?TUVWXY?

Output
ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRZTUVWXYS
Input
WELCOMETOCODEFORCESROUNDTHREEHUNDREDANDSEVENTYTWO

Output
-1
Input
??????????????????????????

Output
MNBVCXZLKJHGFDSAQPWOEIRUYT
Input
AABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVW??M

Output
-1


-----Note-----

In the first sample case, ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRZTUVWXYS is a valid answer beacuse it contains a substring of length 26 (the whole string in this case) which contains all the letters of the English alphabet exactly once. Note that there are many possible solutions, such as ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ or ABCEDFGHIJKLMNOPQRZTUVWXYS.

In the second sample case, there are no missing letters. In addition, the given string does not have a substring of length 26 that contains all the letters of the alphabet, so the answer is  - 1.

In the third sample case, any string of length 26 that contains all letters of the English alphabet fits as an answer.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout


Now solve the problem and return the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find if a string with missing letters (represented by '?') can be filled to contain a substring of length 26 where each letter A-Z appears exactly once.
2. If such a substring exists, we output one possible solution; otherwise, output -1.
3. The substring must be contiguous and of length exactly 26.

## Key Insights

1. The string must have at least 26 characters to have a substring of length 26.
2. If the string length < 26, return -1.
3. We need to find at least one contiguous substring of length 26 that contains all 26 letters exactly once.
4. For each possible position of a 26-letter substring, we need to check if we can fill the '?' characters to form a permutation of all 26 letters.
5. The filled substring must contain exactly one of each letter A-Z.

## Algorithm Steps

1. Check if string length < 26 → return -1
2. For each possible starting position i (from 0 to len(s)-26):
   - Extract substring of length 26 starting at i
   - Check if all characters in this substring are either known letters or '?' 
   - Check if the known letters in this substring don't have duplicates
   - If valid, try to construct a permutation of all 26 letters
3. To construct the permutation:
   - Count frequency of each letter in the known characters of the 26-length window
   - For each of the 26 letters, assign it if it's not already present in the known letters
   - For '?' characters, assign the missing letters
4. Once we find a valid window, construct the full string with the rest filled appropriately

## Implementation Strategy

For each window of length 26:
1. Extract the characters
2. Count the frequency of known letters
3. Check if any letter appears more than once in the known part → invalid
4. Check if any letter is missing from the 26 letters → we need to assign missing letters to '?' positions
5. If valid, create a solution by assigning missing letters to '?' positions in this window, and fill the rest of the string with any valid assignment.

Let me implement this:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If string length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # We need to find a window of 26 consecutive characters that can form a permutation of all 26 letters
    alphabet = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"
    
    # Try each possible starting position for 26-length substring
    for start in range(n - 25):  # from 0 to n-26 (inclusive)
        window = s[start:start+26]
        
        # Count frequency of each letter in the window
        char_count = {}
        valid = True
        
        # Check if any letter appears more than once in the known positions
        for char in window:
            if char != '?':
                char_count[char] = char_count.get(char, 0) + 1
                if char_count[char] > 1:
                    valid = False
                    break
        
        if not valid:
            continue
        
        # Check if we have all 26 letters (we need exactly one of each)
        # We'll need to assign the missing letters to the '?' positions
        missing_letters = []
        
        # Find which letters are missing from the 26 letters
        for letter in alphabet:
            if letter not in char_count or char_count[letter] == 0:
                missing_letters.append(letter)
        
        # Check if we have exactly 26 - len(missing_letters) missing letters
        # Actually, we need to know how many positions are '?' and how many letters we need
        q_count = 0
        for char in window:
            if char == '?':
                q_count += 1
        
        # We need to assign missing_letters to the '?' positions
        # The number of missing letters should equal the number of '?' positions
        if len(missing_letters) != q_count:
            continue
        
        # We found a valid window! Now construct the solution
        
        # Create the window with the missing letters filled in
        result_window = list(window)
        letter_idx = 0
        
        for i in range(26):
            if window[i] == '?':
                result_window[i] = missing_letters[letter_idx]
                letter_idx += 1
        
        # Create the full result string
        result = list(s)
        
        # Fill only the 26-character window we found
        for i in range(26):
            result[start + i] = result_window[i]
        
        # The rest of the positions can be filled with any letters, but we need to ensure 
        # we don't create conflicts - however, since we only care about having one valid 26-letter substring,
        # we can fill the rest with any letters.
        
        # Fill the rest of the positions with 'A' for simplicity
        # But we need to be careful - if there are constraints in other positions, we might have issues.
        # However, the problem only requires one valid 26-letter substring.
        
        # Since we only need to find one solution, we can just fill the rest arbitrarily.
        # The only constraint is that we don't break the existing known letters.
        
        # Our work is done for the 26-letter window. The rest can be filled with any letters.
        # We'll fill with 'A' to keep it simple.
        
        # Actually, we should fill the rest with any valid letters - we can just use a simple pattern.
        # But we don't need to worry about other windows since we only need one valid 26-letter substring.
        
        # Let's just fill the rest with 'A'
        # However, we should check if we can fill the rest more simply.
        
        # Since we only need one valid solution, we can just return this result
        return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid window found
    return -1

# Handle the case where no valid window exists
result = solve()
if result == -1:
    print(-1)
else:
    print(result)
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find if we can replace all '?' characters with letters so that the resulting string has a substring of length 26 where each letter of the English alphabet appears exactly once.

2. A "nice" word must have at least one contiguous substring of length 26 that contains all 26 letters exactly once.

3. If such a substring exists, we need to output one valid replacement. Otherwise, output -1.

## Key Insights

1. The string must have at least 26 characters to have a substring of length 26.

2. We need to find one contiguous substring of length 26 that contains all 26 letters exactly once.

3. For each possible position of a 26-length substring, we need to check if we can replace the '?' characters such that all 26 letters appear exactly once.

4. If we find such a valid substring, we can fill the rest of the string with any letters (but we must maintain the original characters and constraints).

## Algorithm

1. If the string length is less than 26, return -1.

2. For each starting position i (from 0 to n-26), check if we can form a substring of length 26 from position i to i+25 that contains all 26 letters exactly once.

3. To check if we can form a valid 26-letter substring at position i:
   - Count the frequency of each letter in the substring (ignoring '?' for now)
   - For each letter that already appears, it must appear exactly once
   - The number of '?' characters must be exactly equal to the number of letters we need to add
   - The remaining letters must be exactly 26 total

4. If we find a valid position, we can construct the answer by:
   - Filling the 26-letter substring with the required letters
   - Keeping other characters as they are or filling '?' with any letter

5. We only need to find one valid solution.

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If string is shorter than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # For each possible starting position of a 26-length substring
    for start in range(n - 25):  # start from 0 to n-26
        substring = s[start:start+26]
        
        # Count frequency of existing letters in this substring
        letter_count = {}
        question_marks = 0
        
        for char in substring:
            if char == '?':
                question_marks += 1
            else:
                letter_count[char] = letter_count.get(char, 0) + 1
        
        # Check if we can make all letters appear exactly once
        # We need exactly 26 distinct letters, each appearing once
        # Currently we have some letters already present
        
        # Count how many letters are already present (without duplicates)
        existing_letters = set()
        for char in substring:
            if char != '?':
                existing_letters.add(char)
        
        # Check if we have too many occurrences of any letter
        for count in letter_count.values():
            if count > 1:
                break
        else:  # No break happened, all counts are 1
            # Check if we have exactly the right number of letters to be filled
            # We need 26 distinct letters, we already have len(existing_letters) letters
            # We need to fill (26 - len(existing_letters)) letters with the '?'s
            letters_needed = 26 - len(existing_letters)
            
            # We need exactly 'letters_needed' question marks to fill in the missing letters
            if question_marks == letters_needed:
                # We can create a valid substring here
                # Build the answer string
                result = list(s)
                
                # Fill the 26-letter substring with the required letters
                # Start with the current letters in their positions
                current_letters = []
                for i in range(26):
                    char = substring[i]
                    if char != '?':
                        current_letters.append(char)
                    else:
                        # We'll fill this with a letter that's missing
                        # We need to make sure all 26 letters are present
                        pass
                
                # Create a list of all 26 letters we need
                all_letters = set('ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ')
                present_letters = set()
                for char in substring:
                    if char != '?':
                        present_letters.add(char)
                
                # Fill the missing letters
                missing_letters = list(all_letters - present_letters)
                
                # Create the 26-letter substring with all letters
                filled_substring = []
                position_map = {}
                for i in range(26):
                    char = substring[i]
                    if char != '?':
                        filled_substring.append(char)
                        position_map[i] = char
                    else:
                        # Find the next missing letter
                        next_missing = None
                        for missing in missing_letters:
                            if missing not in filled_substring:
                                next_missing = missing
                                break
                        if next_missing is None:
                            # Should not happen
                            pass
                        filled_substring.append(next_missing)
                        # Remove this letter from missing letters
                        missing_letters.remove(next_missing)
                
                # Update result with the filled substring
                for i in range(26):
                    result[start + i] = filled_substring[i]
                
                return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid 26-letter substring found
    return -1

print(solve())
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If string is shorter than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # For each possible starting position of a 26-length substring
    for start in range(n - 25):  # start from 0 to n-26
        substring = s[start:start+26]
        
        # Count frequency of existing letters in this substring
        letter_count = {}
        question_marks = 0
        
        for char in substring:
            if char == '?':
                question_marks += 1
            else:
                letter_count[char] = letter_count.get(char, 0) + 1
        
        # Check if we have any letter appearing more than once
        for count in letter_count.values():
            if count > 1:
                break
        else:  # No break happened, all counts are 1 or 0
            # Count how many distinct letters we already have
            distinct_letters = set()
            for char in substring:
                if char != '?':
                    distinct_letters.add(char)
            
            # We need exactly 26 distinct letters, we already have len(distinct_letters)
            # We need to fill (26 - len(distinct_letters)) letters
            letters_needed = 26 - len(distinct_letters)
            
            # We need exactly 'letters_needed' question marks
            if question_marks == letters_needed:
                # We can create a valid substring here
                # Build the answer string
                result = list(s)
                
                # Create the 26-letter substring with all 26 letters
                all_letters = set('ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ')
                present_letters = set()
                for char in substring:
                    if char != '?':
                        present_letters.add(char)
                
                # Get the missing letters
                missing_letters = list(all_letters - present_letters)
                
                # Create the filled substring
                filled_substring = []
                for i in range(26):
                    char = substring[i]
                    if char != '?':
                        filled_substring.append(char)
                    else:
                        # Take the next missing letter
                        next_missing = missing_letters.pop(0)
                        filled_substring.append(next_missing)
                
                # Update result with the filled substring
                for i in range(26):
                    result[start + i] = filled_substring[i]
                
                return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid 26-letter substring found
    return -1

print(solve())
```

                        # Take the next missing letter
                        next_missing = missing_letters.pop(0)
                        filled_substring.append(next_missing)
                
                # Update result with the filled substring
                for i in range(26):
                    result[start + i] = filled_substring[i]
                
                return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid 26-letter substring found
    return -1

print(solve())

-----Output-----

Otherwise, print a string which denotes a possible nice word that ZS the Coder learned. This string should match the string from the input, except for the question marks replaced with uppercase English letters.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find if we can replace all '?' characters in a string to make it "nice"
2. A word is "nice" if it contains a substring of length 26 where each letter A-Z appears exactly once
3. If possible, we return one such valid string; otherwise return -1

## Key Insights

1. The string must have at least 26 characters to have a substring of length 26
2. We need to find a contiguous substring of length 26 that contains all 26 letters exactly once
3. For each possible position of a 26-letter substring, we need to check if we can fill the missing letters (the '?' characters) to make it contain all 26 letters exactly once
4. If we find such a substring, we can construct the full string by extending the valid 26-letter substring and filling other '?' characters appropriately

## Algorithm Steps

1. First check if the string length is less than 26 → return -1
2. For each possible starting position of a 26-length substring (from 0 to n-26):
   - Extract the 26-character window
   - Check if we can fill all '?' in this window to contain all 26 letters exactly once
   - To do this, we need to make sure:
     - All non-'?' characters in the window are distinct and cover all 26 letters
     - The remaining positions (which are '?') can be filled with missing letters
     - We can easily assign missing letters to complete the set
3. If we find a valid 26-letter substring, construct the full answer
4. If no valid window is found, return -1

## Detailed Implementation

For a window to be valid:
- All non-'?' characters must be distinct
- All non-'?' characters must be from A-Z
- The total count of letters in the window must be 26 with no duplicates

Wait, I need to be more careful. The requirement is that each letter appears exactly once in the 26-letter substring.

So in the 26-letter window:
- We need exactly 26 distinct letters, each appearing exactly once
- For each position that is '?', we can assign any letter that is missing from the current set

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If string length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Try each possible starting position for a 26-letter substring
    for start in range(n - 25):  # start from 0 to n-26
        # Extract the 26-character window
        window = s[start:start+26]
        
        # Check if we can make this window contain all 26 letters exactly once
        # First, collect all non-'?' characters in the window
        present_chars = set()
        has_question_marks = False
        
        for char in window:
            if char == '?':
                has_question_marks = True
            else:
                if char in present_chars:
                    # Duplicate character found - impossible
                    break
                present_chars.add(char)
        else:
            # No duplicates found, check if we have exactly 26 distinct letters
            # We need exactly 26 letters, so we need 26 - len(present_chars) missing letters
            # But we have question marks which we can use to fill them
            missing_chars_needed = 26 - len(present_chars)
            if has_question_marks:
                # We have question marks, we can fill them with missing letters
                # Check if the number of question marks is at least missing_chars_needed
                question_count = window.count('?')
                if question_count >= missing_chars_needed:
                    # We can fill the missing letters
                    # Now construct the full result string
                    result = list(s)
                    
                    # Fill the 26-letter window with the correct letters
                    # First fill the existing letters that are not '?' 
                    # Then fill the '?' positions with missing letters
                    window_chars = list(window)
                    missing_set = set('ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ') - present_chars
                    
                    # Fill the '?' positions with missing letters
                    missing_list = list(missing_set)
                    missing_idx = 0
                    
                    for i in range(26):
                        if window_chars[i] == '?':
                            window_chars[i] = missing_list[missing_idx]
                            missing_idx += 1
                    
                    # Now fill the rest of the string - for positions outside this window
                    # We can fill '?' with any letter as long as we don't break the nice property
                    # But we only need one valid solution, so we can fill the rest arbitrarily
                    # We'll use the same pattern, just make sure we don't create duplicates in this window
                    # Actually, for positions outside, we just replace '?' with any letter
                    # For simplicity, we can just fill with letters from A-Z
                    for i in range(n):
                        if i < start or i >= start + 26:
                            # Outside the window, just replace '?' with any letter
                            # We'll use a simple pattern, but we don't need to worry about it
                            # as long as some 26-letter substring has all letters
                            if result[i] == '?':
                                # Add any letter, for simplicity use 'A'
                                result[i] = 'A'
                    
                    # Actually, we need to be more careful about the non-window parts
                    # But the problem only requires one nice substring, not all substrings
                    # So we can fill non-window parts arbitrarily
                    # We just need to ensure the 26-letter window is complete
                    
                    # Fix the 26-letter window in result
                    for i in range(26):
                        result[start + i] = window_chars[i]
                    
                    # We just need to make sure we have a valid answer
                    # The rest can be filled with any letters
                    
                    # But we need to check that the window actually has all 26 letters
                    # Let's verify
                    window_result = ''.join(result[start:start+26])
                    window_set = set(window_result)
                    
                    if len(window_set) == 26:
                        # This is a valid solution
                        print(''.join(result))
                        return
                else:
                    # Not enough question marks to fill all missing letters
                    continue
            else:
                # No question marks, so we need exactly 26 distinct letters
                if len(present_chars) == 26:
                    # We have all 26 letters already
                    # Now construct the full answer
                    result = list(s)
                    # Fill the 26-letter window - it's already correct
                    for i in range(26):
                        result[start + i] = window[i]
                    
                    # Fill other '?' with any letter (simplest: use 'A')
                    for i in range(n):
                        if i < start or i >= start + 26:
                            if result[i] == '?':
                                result[i] = 'A'
                    
                    print(''.join(result))
                    return
                else:
                    # We have fewer than 26 letters, even if no question marks
                    continue
    
    # No valid window found
    print(-1)
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find if we can replace all '?' characters with letters so that there exists a substring of length 26 where each letter of the alphabet appears exactly once.
2. If such a substring exists, we output one possible solution; otherwise, output -1.
3. The substring of length 26 must contain exactly one occurrence of each letter A-Z.

## Key Insights

1. The string must have at least 26 characters to have a substring of length 26.
2. We need to check if there exists any contiguous substring of length 26 that can be made to contain all 26 letters exactly once.
3. For each possible starting position of a 26-character substring, we need to check if we can assign letters to the '?' positions such that we get all 26 letters.

## Algorithm Steps

1. First, check if the string length is less than 26 → return -1.
2. For each possible starting position i (0 to n-26) of a 26-character substring:
   - Check if we can form a valid alphabet substring starting at i
   - For positions in the substring [i, i+25]:
     - If the character is already a letter, it must match what we need
     - If it's '?', we can assign it freely
   - We need to check if there are no conflicting letters already present
   - We need to ensure we can assign the missing letters to complete the alphabet

3. To check if a substring can be valid:
   - Count existing letters in the substring
   - If any letter appears more than once, it's invalid
   - If any letter appears twice (due to fixed positions), we need to check if the count constraint is violated
   - We need to ensure we have at least one position for each letter in the 26-letter set
   - For each letter we need, we must have at least one '?' or a position that can accept it

4. If we find a valid substring, we construct the answer by:
   - Keeping fixed letters as they are
   - Replacing '?' with letters to form a complete alphabet in that substring
   - For positions outside the valid 26-substring, just replace '?' with arbitrary letters (we can use a pattern)

5. If no valid substring is found, return -1.

## Implementation Details

For each possible 26-character window:
- Count the frequency of each letter in the window
- Check if any letter appears more than once (this would be invalid)
- Check if any required letter is missing (we need all 26 letters)
- The positions that are '?' can be used to complete the missing letters

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # For each possible starting position of a 26-length substring
    for start in range(n - 25):
        # Extract the substring from start to start+25
        substring = s[start:start+26]
        
        # Count frequency of each letter in this substring
        freq = {}
        has_conflict = False
        
        # Check if any letter appears more than once in the fixed positions
        for i, char in enumerate(substring):
            if char != '?':
                if char in freq:
                    # Conflict: same letter appears twice
                    has_conflict = True
                    break
                freq[char] = 1
        
        # If there's a conflict, this substring can't be valid
        if has_conflict:
            continue
            
        # Check if we have all 26 letters needed
        # We need to see if we can complete the alphabet with the remaining positions
        # The remaining positions are the '?' positions in this substring
        required_letters = set('ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ')
        existing_letters = set(freq.keys())
        
        # Letters we need to add
        missing_letters = required_letters - existing_letters
        
        # Check if we have enough '?' positions to fill the missing letters
        q_positions = []
        for i, char in enumerate(substring):
            if char == '?':
                q_positions.append(i)
        
        # We need to assign exactly one letter to each missing letter
        if len(missing_letters) > len(q_positions):
            continue
        
        # We have enough positions, so we can complete this substring
        # Now build the full result string
        result = list(s)
        
        # Fill the substring with the required letters
        letter_index = 0
        for i, char in enumerate(substring):
            if char == '?':
                # Assign the next missing letter
                if letter_index < len(missing_letters):
                    result[start + i] = missing_letters[letter_index]
                    letter_index += 1
                else:
                    # This shouldn't happen since we checked the count
                    pass
            # If char is not '?', keep it as is (it's already handled in frequency check)
        
        # The remaining positions (outside the 26-substring) can be filled with any letters
        # We can use a simple pattern for the rest
        for i in range(n):
            if i < start or i >= start + 26:
                if s[i] == '?':
                    # We can assign any letter, let's just use a simple pattern
                    # For example, cycle through A-Z
                    result[i] = chr(ord('A') + (i % 26))
        
        # We have a valid solution
        return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid substring found
    return -1

# Solve and output
result = solve()
print(result)
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # For each possible starting position of a 26-length substring
    for start in range(n - 25):

        substring = s[start:start+26]
        
        # Count frequency of each letter in this substring
        freq = {}
        has_conflict = False
        has_fixed_letter = {}
        
        # First, check if any letter appears more than once in fixed positions
        for i, char in enumerate(substring):
            if char != '?':
                if char in freq:
                    has_conflict = True
                    break
                freq[char] = 1
                has_fixed_letter[char] = True
        
        # If there's a conflict, skip this substring
        if has_conflict:
            continue
            
        # We have all unique letters in fixed positions
        # Check what letters we need to add
        required_letters = set('ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ')
        existing_letters = set(freq.keys())
        missing_letters = required_letters - existing_letters

        q_positions = []
        for i, char in enumerate(substring):
            if char == '?':
                q_positions.append(i)

        if len(missing_letters) > len(q_positions):
            continue

        # Build the result string
        result = list(s)
        
        # Fill the substring with the required letters
        letter_index = 0
        for i, char in enumerate(substring):
            if char == '?':
                # Assign the next missing letter
                result[start + i] = missing_letters[letter_index]
                letter_index += 1
            # If char is not '?' and not in freq, this should not happen due to our check

        for i in range(n):

                # This position is outside our substring and is '?' 
                # We can assign any letter, let's use a simple pattern
                result[i] = chr(ord('A') + (i % 26))
        
        return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid substring found
    return -1
```

        for i in range(n):
            if s[i] == '?' and not (start <= i < start + 26):
                # This position is outside our substring and is '?' 
                # We can assign any letter, let's use a simple pattern
                result[i] = chr(ord('A') + (i % 26))
        
        return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid substring found
    return -1

# Solve and output
result = solve()
print(result)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find if we can replace all question marks in a string so that it contains a substring of length 26 where each letter A-Z appears exactly once.
2. If possible, output one such valid string; otherwise output -1.
3. The substring of length 26 must contain each letter exactly once (a permutation of A-Z).

## Key Insights

1. **Length requirement**: The string must have at least 26 characters. If |s| < 26, return -1.
2. **Substring requirement**: We need to find a contiguous substring of length 26 that contains all 26 letters exactly once.
3. **Missing letters**: Question marks can be replaced with any letter to satisfy the requirement.
4. **Strategy**: We need to check if there exists a substring of length 26 where we can assign letters such that all 26 letters appear exactly once.

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. **Check length**: If string length < 26, return -1.
2. **Find valid windows**: For each possible window of length 26, check if we can make it contain all 26 letters.
3. **Check feasibility**: For each window of length 26:
   - Count existing letters (non-? characters)
   - Count question marks
   - Check if we have enough "free" positions to place the missing letters
   - Verify that no letter appears more than once in the window
4. **Construct solution**: Once we find a valid window, fill in the missing letters appropriately.

## Implementation Plan

For each window of length 26:
- Count frequency of each letter in the window
- Count number of question marks
- Check if the number of missing letters (26 - number of distinct non-? letters) equals the number of question marks
- Check that no letter appears more than once in the window (already enforced by the condition above)

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If string length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # We need to check all windows of length 26
    alphabet = 'ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ'
    
    # Try each possible starting position for a window of length 26
    for start in range(n - 25):
        window = s[start:start + 26]
        
        # Count frequency of each letter in the window
        freq = {}
        question_marks_count = 0
        
        for char in window:
            if char == '?':
                question_marks_count += 1
            else:
                freq[char] = freq.get(char, 0) + 1
        
        # Check if we have duplicates in non-? positions
        has_duplicate = False
        for count in freq.values():
            if count > 1:
                has_duplicate = True
                break
        
        if has_duplicate:
            continue
            
        # Calculate how many letters we need to add
        # We need 26 distinct letters, we already have len(freq) distinct letters
        letters_needed = 26 - len(freq)
        
        # Check if we have enough question marks
        if question_marks_count < letters_needed:
            continue
            
        # We found a valid window - construct the answer
        result = list(s)
        
        # Fill the question marks in this window with missing letters
        missing_letters = []
        used = set()
        
        # First, add all letters that are already present
        for i in range(26):
            char = alphabet[i]
            if char in freq and freq[char] > 0:
                # We already have this letter, just make sure it's counted correctly
                pass
        
        # Generate the missing letters (the ones we need to add)
        all_letters = set(alphabet)
        existing_letters = set(freq.keys())
        missing_set = all_letters - existing_letters
        
        # Fill the question marks with these missing letters
        for i in range(start, start + 26):
            if s[i] == '?':
                # Find the first missing letter
                for missing_char in missing_set:
                    result[i] = missing_char
                    missing_set.remove(missing_char)
                    break
        
        # We have successfully constructed a valid string
        print(''.join(result))
        return
    
    # No valid window found
    print(-1)

solve()
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find if we can replace all '?' characters with letters such that there exists a substring of length 26 where each letter of English alphabet appears exactly once.
2. If such a substring exists, we output one possible valid string; otherwise, output -1.
3. The substring must contain all 26 letters exactly once (a permutation of "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ").

## Key Insights

1. If the string length is less than 26, it's impossible → return -1.
2. We need to check if there exists any contiguous substring of length 26 that can be made to contain all 26 letters exactly once.
3. For each possible starting position of a 26-letter substring, we need to:
   - Check if we can assign letters to make it contain all 26 letters
   - The existing letters in that substring must not conflict with our requirement
   - The '?' positions can be filled with any letters

## Algorithm Steps

1. Check if length < 26 → return -1
2. For each possible window of length 26 in the string:
   - Create a character frequency count of the current window
   - Count how many positions have fixed letters (non-?) that are already present
   - Check if any letter appears more than once in the window (impossible)
   - For each letter in the alphabet, check if it's already in the window or if we can assign it to a '?' position
   - Count how many letters need to be placed and how many '?' positions we have
3. If we find a valid window, construct the answer by filling '?' with appropriate letters.

Wait, let me reconsider. Actually, we need to ensure that in the 26-letter window:
- All 26 letters must appear exactly once
- For each letter that's already in the window (not '?'), it must appear exactly once
- For letters not present in the window, we need to assign them to '?' positions

Let me refine:

For a window of length 26:
1. Count frequency of each letter in the window (excluding '?')
2. If any letter appears more than once, this window is invalid
3. If any letter in the alphabet is missing from the window, we need to assign it to a '?' position
4. The number of missing letters must equal the number of '?' positions in the window

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # All letters from A to Z
    letters = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"
    
    # Try each possible window of length 26
    for start in range(n - 25):
        # Extract the current window of length 26
        window = s[start:start + 26]
        
        # Count frequency of each letter in the window (ignoring '?')
        letter_count = {}
        question_marks = 0
        
        for char in window:
            if char == '?':
                question_marks += 1
            else:
                letter_count[char] = letter_count.get(char, 0) + 1
        
        # Check if any letter appears more than once
        for count in letter_count.values():
            if count > 1:
                break
        else:  # No break means all counts are 1
            # Check if we have exactly 26 distinct letters in the window
            # We need to check if we can assign all missing letters to '?' positions
            
            # Get the letters that are already present
            present_letters = set(letter_count.keys())
            
            # Letters that are missing from the alphabet
            missing_letters = []
            for letter in letters:
                if letter not in present_letters:
                    missing_letters.append(letter)
            
            # We need to place all missing letters in the '?' positions
            # The number of missing letters must equal the number of '?' positions
            if len(missing_letters) == question_marks:
                # We can construct a valid solution - let's build the result string
                result = list(s)
                
                # Fill the current window with proper letters
                # First, fill in the letters that are already there
                for i in range(start, start + 26):
                    if result[i] != '?':
                        continue
                    # This position is '?' - we need to assign a missing letter
                    # We'll assign in order of the missing letters
                    pass
                
                # Actually, let's build the entire string and just fill one valid window
                # We'll create a new string and then only modify the current window
                new_string = list(s)
                
                # First, make sure the window contains all letters
                # We'll assign the missing letters to '?' positions
                
                # Mark which positions in the window are already filled
                window_chars = list(window)
                missing_positions = []
                
                # Find the positions that are '?'
                for i in range(26):
                    if window[i] == '?':
                        missing_positions.append(i)
                
                # Assign the missing letters to these positions
                missing_letters_copy = missing_letters[:]
                for i in range(len(missing_positions)):
                    pos = missing_positions[i]
                    letter_to_place = missing_letters_copy.pop(0)
                    window_chars[pos] = letter_to_place
                
                # But we have to avoid conflicts - we already ensured no duplicates
                # So this should work
                
                # Replace the current window in the result
                for i in range(26):
                    new_string[start + i] = window_chars[i]
                
                # The rest of the string remains unchanged (we don't need to modify them)
                # But we need to check if this is actually valid - let's verify the window
                # We already ensured no duplicates and all letters are covered
                
                # Return this solution
                return ''.join(new_string)
    
    # If no valid window found
    return -1

print(solve())
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    n = len(s)
    
    # If length is less than 26, impossible
    if n < 26:
        return -1
    
    # All letters from A to Z
    letters = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"
    
    # Try each possible window of length 26
    for start in range(n - 25):
        # Extract the current window of length 26
        window = s[start:start + 26]
        
        # Count frequency of each letter in the window (ignoring '?')
        letter_count = {}
        question_marks = 0
        
        for char in window:
            if char == '?':
                question_marks += 1
            else:
                letter_count[char] = letter_count.get(char, 0) + 1
        
        # Check if any letter appears more than once
        for count in letter_count.values():
            if count > 1:
                break
        else:  # No break means all counts are 1
            # We have a valid character set, now check if we can cover all 26 letters
            # Count how many letters are missing from the alphabet
            present_letters = set(letter_count.keys())
            missing_letters = []
            for letter in letters:
                if letter not in present_letters:
                    missing_letters.append(letter)
            
            # We need exactly as many missing letters as we have question marks
            if len(missing_letters) == question_marks:
                # We can construct a valid solution - build it
                result = list(s)
                
                # For the current window, replace '?' with missing letters
                # We'll process from left to right
                window_list = list(window)
                missing_to_assign = missing_letters[:]
                
                for i in range(26):
                    if window_list[i] == '?':
                        # Assign the next missing letter
                        window_list[i] = missing_to_assign.pop(0)
                
                # Update the result string for this window
                for i in range(26):
                    result[start + i] = window_list[i]
                
                return ''.join(result)
    
    # No valid window found
    return -1

print(solve())
```

```python
else:

    result = list(s)
    
    for start in range(n - 25):
        # Extract the current window of length 26
        window = s[start:start + 26]

        valid = True
        for count in letter_count.values():
            if count > 1:
                valid = False
                break
```
